# Proposed OEIS comments: A299735 to A302950

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A299735

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A299735>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8811](../papers/08501-09000/08811-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 8*a(n-1) -4*a(n-2) -13*a(n-3) -6*a(n-4) +5*a(n-5) +23*a(n-6) -20*a(n-7) -12*a(n-8)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A299737

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A299737>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10749](../papers/10501-11000/10749-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 79 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 100 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 79, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A300212

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A300212>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [230](../papers/00001-00500/00230-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 89 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 89, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A301783

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A301783>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11054](../papers/11001-11500/11054-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 46 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 51 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 46, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A301921

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A301921>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [89](../papers/00001-00500/00089-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: Let k be a positive integer. The sequence obtained by reducing a(n) modulo k is eventually periodic with the period dividing phi(k) = A000010(k). For example, modulo 9 we obtain the sequence [0, 1, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, 3, 1, 6, 4, 0, 7, ...] with an apparent period of 6 = phi(9) beginning at n = 2. Cf. A004123. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. Under t = e^x - 1 the entry's e.g.f. becomes an ordinary power series with integer coefficients; that integrality kills all but finitely many terms of the Stirling expansion of a(n) modulo k, and what is left is an integer combination of the sequences n -> j^n, each eventually periodic mod k with period dividing phi(k). - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A302524

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A302524>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10800](../papers/10501-11000/10800-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 72 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 72 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 72, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
